\bot&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha\fatsemi\beta}(x)=\bot\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\fatsemi\beta,\psi,S\cup\{x\})(s)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha\fatsemi\beta}(x)=\mathsf{fork}\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\fatsemi\beta,\psi,S\cup\{x\})^{\dagger}(\left\llbracket\lambda_{\alpha\fatsemi\beta}(x)\right\rrbracket{act}(s))&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha\fatsemi\beta}(x)\in\mathsk{act}\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\fatsemi\beta,\psi\land\llparenthesis x=\left\llbracket\lambda_{\alpha\fatsemi\beta}(x)\right\rrbracket(s)\rrparenthesis,S\cup\{x\})(s)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha\fatsemi\beta}(x)\in\mathsk{test}\\
\bot&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha\fatsemi\beta}(x)=\bot\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\fatsemi\beta,\psi,S\cup\{x\})(s)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha\fatsemi\beta}(x)=\mathsf{fork}\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta_{\psi},\mathsf{true},\{x\})^{\dagger}(\left\llbracket\lambda_{\beta_{\psi}}(x)\right\rrbracket{act}(s))&\text{if}~\lambda_{\beta_{\psi}}(x)\in\mathsk{act}\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta_{\psi},\llparenthesis x=\left\llbracket\lambda_{\beta_{\psi}}(x)\right\rrbracket(s)\rrparenthesis,\{x\})(s)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\beta_{\psi}}(x)\in\mathsk{test}\\
\bot&\text{if}~\lambda_{\beta_{\psi}}(x)=\bot\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta_{\psi},\mathsf{true},\{x\})(s)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\beta_{\psi}}(x)=\mathsf{fork}\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle=\bignd_{x\in(\beta,\mathsf{true},\emptyset)}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\mathsf{true},\{x\})^{\dagger}(\left\llbracket\lambda_{\beta}(x)\right\rrbracket{act}(s))&\text{if}~\lambda_{\beta}(x)\in\mathsk{act}\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\llparenthesis x=\left\llbracket\lambda_{\beta}(x)\right\rrbracket(s)\rrparenthesis,\{x\})(s)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\beta}(x)\in\mathsk{test}\\
\bot&\text{if}~\lambda_{\beta}(x)=\bot\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\mathsf{true},\{x\})(s)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\beta}(x)=\mathsf{fork}\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\mathsf{true},\emptyset)(s)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\mathsf{true},\emptyset)^{\dagger}(\eta(s))

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\mathsf{true},\emptyset)^{\dagger}(\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi,S)(s))

Now suppose
{}^{\ast}(\alpha,\psi,S)\neq\emptyset
. By
Lemma

64
, we know that
(\alpha\fatsemi_{f}\beta,\psi,S)=(\alpha,\psi,S)\neq\emptyset
. Given this, we have:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\fatsemi\beta,\psi,S)(s)

\displaystyle=\bignd_{x\in\mathsf{next}(\alpha\fatsemi\beta,\psi,S)}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\fatsemi\beta,\psi,S\cup\{x\})^{\dagger}(\left\llbracket\lambda_{\alpha\fatsemi\beta}(x)\right\rrbracket{act}(s))&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha\fatsemi\beta}(x)\in\mathsk{act}\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\fatsemi\beta,\psi\land\llparenthesis x=\left\llbracket\lambda_{\alpha\fatsemi\beta}(x)\right\rrbracket(s)\rrparenthesis,S\cup\{x\})(s)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha\fatsemi\beta}(x)\in\mathsk{test}\\
\bot&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha\fatsemi\beta}(x)=\bot\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\fatsemi\beta,\psi,S\cup\{x\})(s)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha\fatsemi\beta}(x)=\mathsf{fork}\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle=\bignd_{x\in\mathsf{next}(\alpha,\psi,S)}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\fatsemi\beta,\psi,S\cup\{x\})^{\dagger}(\left\llbracket\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\right\rrbracket{act}(s))&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\in\mathsk{act}\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\fatsemi\beta,\psi\land\llparenthesis x=\left\llbracket\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\right\rrbracket(s)\rrparenthesis,S\cup\{x\})(s)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\in\mathsk{test}\\
\bot&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)=\bot\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\fatsemi\beta,\psi,S\cup\{x\})(s)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)=\mathsf{fork}\end{array}\right.

Clearly
{}^{\ast}(\alpha,\psi,S\cup\{x\})\subset^{\ast}(\alpha,\psi,S)
and
\psi\in\mathsf{valid}_{\alpha}(S\cup\{x\})
, if
x
is not a test node and therefore
\mathsf{valid}_{\alpha}(S\cup\{x\})=\mathsf{valid}_{\alpha}(S)
. So, by the induction hypothesis and monad laws, we get:

\displaystyle=\bignd_{x\in\mathsf{next}(\alpha,\psi,S)}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\mathsf{true},\emptyset)^{\dagger}(\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi,S\cup\{x\})^{\dagger}(\left\llbracket\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\right\rrbracket{act}(s)))&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\in\mathsk{act}\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\fatsemi\beta,\psi\land\llparenthesis x=\left\llbracket\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\right\rrbracket(s)\rrparenthesis,S\cup\{x\})(s)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\in\mathsk{test}\\
\bot&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)=\bot\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\mathsf{true},\emptyset)^{\dagger}(\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi,S\cup\{x\})(s))&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)=\mathsf{fork}\end{array}\right.

We also have that
{}^{\ast}(\alpha,S\cup\{x\},\psi\land\llparenthesis x=\left\llbracket\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\right\rrbracket{test}(s)\rrparenthesis)\subset^{\ast}(\alpha,S,\psi)
. In addition,
\psi\land\llparenthesis x=\left\llbracket\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\right\rrbracket{test}(s)\rrparenthesis\in\mathsf{valid}_{\alpha}(S\cup\{x\})
. So, by the induction hypothesis:

\displaystyle=\bignd_{x\in\mathsf{next}(\alpha,\psi,S)}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\mathsf{true},\emptyset)^{\dagger}(\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi,S\cup\{x\})^{\dagger}(\left\llbracket\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\right\rrbracket{act}(s)))&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\in\mathsk{act}\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\mathsf{true},\emptyset)^{\dagger}(\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi\land\llparenthesis x=\left\llbracket\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\right\rrbracket(s)\rrparenthesis,S\cup\{x\})(s))&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\in\mathsk{test}\\
\bot&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)=\bot\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\mathsf{true},\emptyset)^{\dagger}(\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi,S\cup\{x\})(s))&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)=\mathsf{fork}\end{array}\right.

Finally since
f^{\dagger}(\bot)=\bot
for any
f
, we get:

\displaystyle=\bignd_{x\in\mathsf{next}(\alpha,\psi,S)}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\mathsf{true},\emptyset)^{\dagger}(\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi,S\cup\{x\})^{\dagger}(\left\llbracket\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\right\rrbracket{act}(s)))&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\in\mathsk{act}\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\mathsf{true},\emptyset)^{\dagger}(\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi\land\llparenthesis x=\left\llbracket\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\right\rrbracket(s)\rrparenthesis,S\cup\{x\})(s))&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\in\mathsk{test}\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\mathsf{true},\emptyset)^{\dagger}(\bot)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)=\bot\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\mathsf{true},\emptyset)^{\dagger}(\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi,S\cup\{x\})(s))&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)=\mathsf{fork}\end{array}\right.

Since
f^{\dagger}(X\mathbin{\&}Y)=f^{\dagger}(X)\mathbin{\&}f^{\dagger}(Y)
:

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\mathsf{true},\emptyset)^{\dagger}\left(\bignd_{x\in\mathsf{next}(\alpha,\psi,S)}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi,S\cup\{x\})^{\dagger}(\left\llbracket\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\right\rrbracket{act}(s))&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\in\mathsk{act}\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi\land\llparenthesis x=\left\llbracket\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\right\rrbracket(s)\rrparenthesis,S\cup\{x\})(s)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\in\mathsk{test}\\
\bot&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)=\bot\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi,S\cup\{x\})(s)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)=\mathsf{fork}\end{array}\right.\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\mathsf{true},\emptyset)^{\dagger}\left(\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi,S)(s)\right)\qed

Lemma 66
.

\mathcal{L}(\left\llbracket C_{1};C_{2}\right\rrbracket)=\mathcal{L}(\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}\circ\mathcal{L}(\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket)
.

Proof.

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\left\llbracket C_{1};C_{2}\right\rrbracket)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}(\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket\fatsemi\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket)

By the definition of
\fatsemi
for infinite pomsets:

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}\left(\sup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}\ll\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}\ll\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket}\boldsymbol{\alpha}\fatsemi\boldsymbol{\beta}\right)

Since
\mathcal{L}
is Scott continuous:

\displaystyle=\sup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}\ll\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}\ll\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket}\mathcal{L}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\fatsemi\boldsymbol{\beta})

\displaystyle=\sup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}\ll\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}\ll\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}\ll\boldsymbol{\alpha}\fatsemi\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\boldsymbol{\gamma})

Since
\boldsymbol{\alpha}\fatsemi\boldsymbol{\beta}
is finite, then
\boldsymbol{\alpha}\fatsemi\boldsymbol{\beta}\ll\boldsymbol{\alpha}\fatsemi\boldsymbol{\beta}
and therefore since
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsf{fin}}
is monotone, then
\sup_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}\ll\boldsymbol{\alpha}\fatsemi\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\boldsymbol{\gamma})=\mathcal{L}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\fatsemi\boldsymbol{\beta})
.

\displaystyle=\sup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}\ll\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}\ll\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\fatsemi\boldsymbol{\beta})

\displaystyle=\sup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}\ll\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}\ll\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\{\alpha\fatsemi_{f}\beta\mid\alpha\in\boldsymbol{\alpha},\beta\in\boldsymbol{\beta},f\in\mathsf{copy}_{\alpha,\beta}\})

The set above is an equivalence class, so fixing an arbitrary
\alpha\in\boldsymbol{\alpha}
,
\beta\in\boldsymbol{\beta}
, and
f\in\mathsf{copy}_{\alpha,\beta}
, we can rewrite the expression as follows:

\displaystyle=\sup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}\ll\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}\ll\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsf{fin}}([\alpha\fatsemi_{f}\beta])

\displaystyle=\sup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}\ll\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}\ll\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\fatsemi_{f}\beta,\mathsf{true},\emptyset)

By
Lemma

65
:

\displaystyle=\sup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}\ll\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}\ll\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\mathsf{true},\emptyset)^{\dagger}\circ\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\mathsf{true},\emptyset)

\displaystyle=\sup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}\ll\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}\ll\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsf{fin}}([\beta])^{\dagger}\circ\mathcal{L}_{\mathsf{fin}}([\alpha])

By continuity of Kleisli extension, and since
\boldsymbol{\alpha}=[\alpha]
and
\boldsymbol{\beta}=[\beta]
:

\displaystyle=\left(\sup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}\ll\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\boldsymbol{\beta})\right)^{\dagger}\circ\sup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}\ll\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\boldsymbol{\alpha})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}(\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket)^{\dagger}\circ\mathcal{L}(\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket)\qed

G.4
Properties of Linearization: Guarded Branching

Lemma 67
.

For any
\alpha,\beta\in{\mathsk{lpof}}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\mathsk{act})
,
b\in\mathsk{test}
, and
x\in\mathsk{nodes}\setminus(N_{\alpha}\cup N_{\beta})
:

\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta),\mathsf{true},\emptyset)(s)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\mathsf{true},\emptyset)(s)&\text{if}\ \left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket{test}(s)=1\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\mathsf{true},\emptyset)(s)&\text{if}\ \left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket{test}(s)=0\end{array}\right.

Proof.

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta),\mathsf{true},\emptyset)(s)

\displaystyle={\bignd_{x\in\mathsf{next}(\alpha,\psi,S)}}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta),\mathsf{true},\{x\})^{\dagger}(\left\llbracket\lambda_{\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta)}(x)\right\rrbracket{act}(s))&\text{if}~\lambda_{\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta)}(x)\in\mathsk{act}\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta),\llparenthesis x=\left\llbracket\lambda_{\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta)}(x)\right\rrbracket(s)\rrparenthesis,\{x\})(s)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta)}(x)\in\mathsk{test}\\
\bot&\text{if}~\lambda_{\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta)}(x)=\bot\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta),\mathsf{true},\{x\})(s)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta)}(x)=\mathsf{fork}\end{array}\right.

Since
x
is the root of
\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta)
, then
\mathsf{next}(\alpha,\psi,S)=\{x\}
and
\lambda_{\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta)}(x)\in\mathsk{test}
.

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta),\llparenthesis x=\left\llbracket\lambda_{\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta)}(x)\right\rrbracket(s)\rrparenthesis,\{x\})(s)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta),x,\{x\})(s)&\text{if}\ \left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket{test}(s)=1\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta),\lnot x,\{x\})(s)&\text{if}\ \left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket{test}(s)=0\end{array}\right.

The formula
x
prevents all nodes in
\beta
from being scheduled and similarly
\lnot x
prevents the
\alpha
nodes from being scheduled, so we can remove the
\mathsf{guard}
call to obtain the following:

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\mathsf{true},\emptyset)(s)&\text{if}\ \left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket{test}(s)=\mathsf{true}\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\mathsf{true},\emptyset)(s)&\text{if}\ \left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket{test}(s)=\mathsf{false}\end{array}\right.\qed

Lemma 68
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\left\llbracket\textsf{if}~b~\textsf{then}~C_{1}~\textsf{else}~C_{2}\right\rrbracket)(s)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{L}(\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket)(s)&\text{if}~\left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket{test}(s)=1\\
\mathcal{L}(\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket)(s)&\text{if}~\left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket{test}(s)=0\end{array}\right.

Proof.

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}{\left\llbracket\textsf{if}~b~\textsf{then}~C_{1}~\textsf{else}~C_{2}\right\rrbracket}(s)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}(\mathsf{guard}(b,\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket,\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket))(s)

\displaystyle=\sup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}\ll\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}\ll\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\mathsf{guard}(b,\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}))(s)

\displaystyle=\sup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}\ll\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}\ll\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\{\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta)\mid x\in\mathsk{nodes},\alpha\in\boldsymbol{\alpha},\beta\in\boldsymbol{\beta}\})(s)

\displaystyle=\sup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}\ll\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}\ll\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsf{fin}}([\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta)])(s)

\displaystyle=\sup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}\ll\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}\ll\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta),\mathsf{true},\emptyset)(s)

\displaystyle=\sup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}\ll\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}\ll\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\mathsf{true},\emptyset)(s)&\text{if}\ \left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket{test}(s)=1\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\mathsf{true},\emptyset)(s)&\text{if}\ \left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket{test}(s)=0\end{array}\right.\qquad\text{(By \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{lem:lin-lpo-guard})}

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}\ll\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\boldsymbol{\alpha})(s)&\text{if}\ \left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket{test}(s)=1\\
\sup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}\ll\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\boldsymbol{\beta})(s)&\text{if}\ \left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket{test}(s)=0\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{L}(\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket)(s)&\text{if}\ \left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket{test}(s)=1\\
\mathcal{L}(\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket)(s)&\text{if}\ \left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket{test}(s)=0\end{array}\right.\qed

G.5
Properties of Linearization: While Loops

Lemma 69
.

Let
D(S)
be the dcpo such that
\mathcal{L}\colon{\mathsk{pom}}\to S\to D(S)
, and let
\bot_{\mathsk{pom}}\in{\mathsk{pom}}
be the bottom of the pomset order,
\bot_{D}\in D(S)
be the bottom of
D(S)
, and
\bot_{D}^{\bullet}\colon S\to D(S)
be the bottom of the pointwise order on
D(S)
. Then

\mathcal{L}\left(\Phi^{n}_{\langle C,b\rangle}(\bot_{\mathsk{pom}})\right)=\Psi^{n}_{\langle\mathcal{L}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket),b\rangle}(\bot_{D}^{\bullet})

Proof.

The proof is by induction on
n
. If
n=0
, then we have:

\mathcal{L}\left(\Phi^{0}_{\langle C,b\rangle}(\bot_{\mathsk{pom}})\right)(s)=\mathcal{L}(\bot_{\mathsk{pom}})(s)=\bot_{D}=\bot_{D}^{\bullet}(s)=\Psi^{0}_{\langle\mathcal{L}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket),b\rangle}(\bot_{D}^{\bullet})(s)

Now, suppose the claim holds for
n
, then we have:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\left(\Phi^{n+1}_{\langle C,b\rangle}(\bot_{\mathsk{pom}})\right)(s)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}\left(\Phi_{\langle C,b\rangle}\left(\Phi^{n}_{\langle C,b\rangle}(\bot_{\mathsk{pom}})\right)\right)(s)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}\left(\mathsf{guard}\left(b,\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket\fatsemi\Phi^{n}_{\langle C,b\rangle}(\bot_{\mathsk{pom}}),\left\llbracket\textsf{skip}\right\rrbracket\right)\right)(s)

By
Lemma

67
.

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{L}\left(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket\fatsemi\Phi^{n}_{\langle C,b\rangle}(\bot_{\mathsk{pom}})\right)(s)&\text{if}~\left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket{test}(s)=1\\
\mathcal{L}(\left\llbracket\textsf{skip}\right\rrbracket)(s)&\text{if}~\left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket{test}(s)=0\end{array}\right.

By
Lemmas

62
and
66
.

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{L}\left(\Phi^{n}_{\langle C,b\rangle}(\bot_{\mathsk{pom}})\right)^{\dagger}(\mathcal{L}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)(s))&\text{if}~\left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket{test}(s)=1\\
\eta(s)&\text{if}~\left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket{test}(s)=0\end{array}\right.

By the induction hypothesis.

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\left(\Psi^{n}_{\langle\mathcal{L}{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket},b\rangle}(\bot_{D}^{\bullet})\right)^{\dagger}(\mathcal{L}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)(s))&\text{if}~\left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket{test}(s)=1\\
\eta(s)&\text{if}~\left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket{test}(s)=0\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle=\Psi_{\langle\mathcal{L}{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket},b\rangle}\left(\Psi^{n}_{\langle\mathcal{L}{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket},b\rangle}(\bot_{D}^{\bullet})\right)(s)

\displaystyle=\Psi^{n+1}_{\langle\mathcal{L}{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket},b\rangle}(\bot_{D}^{\bullet})(s)\qed

Lemma 70
.

\mathcal{L}(\left\llbracket\textsf{while}~b~\textsf{do}~C\right\rrbracket)=\mathsf{lfp}\left(\Psi_{\langle\mathcal{L}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket),b\rangle}\right)

where

\Psi_{\langle f,b\rangle}(g)(s)\triangleq\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}g^{\dagger}(f(s))&\text{if}~\left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket{test}(\sigma)=1\\
\eta(s)&\text{if}~\left\llbracket b\right\rrbracket{test}(\sigma)=0\end{array}\right.

Proof.

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\left\llbracket\textsf{while}~b~\textsf{do}~C\right\rrbracket)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}\left(\mathsf{lfp}\left(\Phi_{\langle C,b\rangle}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}\left(\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\Phi^{n}_{\langle C,b\rangle}(\bot_{\mathsk{pom}})\right)

(By the Kleene fixed point theorem)

\displaystyle=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{L}\left(\Phi^{n}_{\langle C,b\rangle}(\bot_{\mathsk{pom}})\right)

(By Scott continuity of
\mathcal{L}
)

\displaystyle=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\Psi^{n}_{\langle\mathcal{L}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket),b\rangle}(\bot_{D}^{\bullet})

(By
Lemma

69
)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{lfp}\left(\Psi_{\langle\mathcal{L}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket),b\rangle}\right)

\displaystyle\text{(By the Kleene fixed point theorem)}\qed

G.6
Main Lemma

See
14

Proof.

Follows immediately from
Lemmas

62
,
66
,
68
and
70

∎

Appendix H
Powerdomains

In this section, we fix our domain of computation to be the Hoare powerdomain
\langle\mathcal{P}(S),\subseteq\rangle
. In particular, this means that suprema are given by set union:
\sup_{i\in I}S_{i}\triangleq\bigcup_{i\in I}S_{i}
.

We begin by defining standard pomset language semantics in
Figure

5
.
Although the semantics is defined using pomsets with tests,
\mathsk{test}_{\mathsf{PL}}=\emptyset
, so the formula of every node in
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
is
\mathsf{true}
. In addition, all pomsets are finite and
\bot
nodes are never introduced, meaning that
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
consists only of actions and fork nodes. We therefore get that the definition sequential composition is the standard one,
e.g.,

[
17
,
49
,
38
,
9
]
.

Lemma 71
.

If
\mathsf{Bot}_{\alpha}=\emptyset
,
\varphi_{\alpha}(x)=\mathsf{true}
for all
x\in N_{\alpha}
, and
f=[\mathsf{true}\mapsto\beta]
, then:

N_{\alpha\fatsemi_{f}\beta}=N_{\alpha}\cup N_{\beta}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathord{<}_{\alpha\fatsemi_{f}\beta}=\mathord{<_{\alpha}}\cup\mathord{<_{\beta}}\cup(N_{\alpha}\times N_{\beta})

Proof.

First note that since
\mathsf{Bot}_{\alpha}=\emptyset
, then
\mathsf{stuck}_{\alpha}=\mathsf{false}
and so
\mathsf{ext}_{\alpha}=N_{\alpha}
. Therefore,
\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}=\{\bigwedge_{x\in N_{\alpha}}\varphi_{\alpha}(x)\}=\{\mathsf{true}\}
. We now show the condition on nodes.

N_{\alpha\fatsemi_{f}\beta}=N_{\alpha}\cup\bigcup_{\psi\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}}N_{f(\psi)}=N_{\alpha}\cup N_{f(\mathsf{true})}=N_{\alpha}\cup N_{\beta}

We now also show the condition on orders:

\displaystyle\mathord{<_{\alpha\fatsemi_{f}\beta}}

\displaystyle=\mathord{<_{\alpha}}\cup\bigcup_{\psi\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}}\left(\mathord{<_{f(\psi)}}\cup\{x\in N_{\alpha}\mid\psi\Rightarrow\varphi_{\alpha}(x)\}\times N_{f(\psi)}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathord{<_{\alpha}}\cup\mathord{<_{f(\mathsf{true})}}\cup\{x\in N_{\alpha}\mid\mathsf{true}\Rightarrow\mathsf{true}\}\times N_{f(\mathsf{true})}

\displaystyle=\mathord{<_{\alpha}}\cup\mathord{<_{\beta}}\cup(N_{\alpha}\times N_{\beta})\qed

Loops are interpreted as the least fixed point of
\Xi_{\langle C,b\rangle}
, which is Scott continuous in the subset
\subseteq
order, since suprema (given by
\cup
) are associative and commutative. The
skip
appears in the false branch to correspond to
\Phi_{\langle C,b\rangle}
.

H.1
Definition of Translation

To define
\mathsf{tr}
, we start with a translation on finite
lpof
s, which additionally takes a formula
\psi
to encode the resolution of tests in the current trace.

\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)\triangleq\langle N_{\psi},<_{\psi},\lambda_{\psi},[-\mapsto\mathsf{true}]\rangle

where
[-\mapsto\mathsf{true}]
is the constant
\mathsf{true}
function and

\displaystyle N_{\psi}

\displaystyle\triangleq\{x\in N_{\alpha}\mid\psi\Rightarrow\varphi_{\alpha}(x)\}

\displaystyle\mathord{<_{\psi}}

\displaystyle\triangleq\mathord{<_{\alpha}}\cap(N_{\psi}\times N_{\psi})

\displaystyle\lambda_{\psi}(x)

\displaystyle\triangleq\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\lambda_{\alpha}(x)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\notin\mathsk{test}\\
\textsf{assume}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\in\mathsk{test}~\text{and}~\psi\Rightarrow x\\
\textsf{assume}~\lnot\lambda_{\alpha}(x)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\in\mathsk{test}~\text{and}~\psi\Rightarrow\lnot x\end{array}\right.

The nodes of
\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)
are those nodes
x\in N_{\alpha}
such that
\psi\Rightarrow\varphi_{\alpha}(x)
, indicating that
x
agrees with the outcomes of the tests described by
\psi
. Test nodes with label
b
are converted into either
\textsf{assume}~b
or
\textsf{assume}~\lnot b
, depending on whether that node is true or false in
\psi
.
We define translation on finite pomsets by taking a union over all branches
\psi\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}
.

\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsf{fin}}([\alpha])\triangleq\{[\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)]\mid\psi\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}\}

Finite translation is monotonic (
Lemma

73
)—
\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\boldsymbol{\alpha})\subseteq\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\boldsymbol{\beta})
if
\boldsymbol{\alpha}\sqsubseteq_{{\mathsk{pom}}}\boldsymbol{\beta}
—therefore we can extend to infinite pomsets with the extension lemma.

\mathsf{tr}(\boldsymbol{\alpha})\triangleq\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsf{fin}}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\alpha})

H.2
Properties of the Translation Function

Lemma 72

(Monotonicity of
\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}
)
.

If
\alpha\sqsubseteq_{\mathsk{lpof}}\alpha^{\prime}
and
\psi\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}
, then
\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)=\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha^{\prime},\psi)

Proof.

We first show that
N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)}=N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha^{\prime},\psi)}
, or in other words, that
\{x\in N_{\alpha}\mid\psi\Rightarrow\varphi_{\alpha}(x)\}=\{x\in N_{\alpha^{\prime}}\mid\psi\Rightarrow\varphi_{\alpha^{\prime}}(x)\}
The forward inclusion is immediate since
N_{\alpha}\subseteq N_{\alpha}^{\prime}
and
\varphi_{\alpha}(x)=\varphi_{\alpha^{\prime}}(x)
for all
x\in N_{\alpha}
. We now show the reverse inclusion. Take any
x\in N_{\alpha^{\prime}}
such that
\psi\Rightarrow\varphi_{\alpha^{\prime}}(x)
. If
x\notin N_{\alpha}
, then by
Lemma

23
,
x\in{\mathsf{Bot}_{\alpha}\mathclose{\uparrow}}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
. This means that there is some
y\in\mathsf{Bot}_{\alpha}
such that
y<_{\alpha^{\prime}}x
, and so
\varphi_{\alpha^{\prime}}(x)\Rightarrow\varphi_{\alpha^{\prime}}(y)\Rightarrow\mathsf{stuck}_{\alpha}
. But this contradicts the fact that
\psi\Rightarrow\varphi_{\alpha^{\prime}}(x)
and
\psi\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}
. Therefore, it must be that
x\in N_{\alpha}
, and so the claim holds.

Now, for the order, we have:

\displaystyle\mathord{<_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)}}

\displaystyle=\mathord{<_{\alpha}}\cap(N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)}\times N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)})

\displaystyle=(\mathord{<_{\alpha^{\prime}}}\cap(N_{\alpha}\times N_{\alpha}))\cap(N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)}\times N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)})

Since
N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)}\subseteq N_{\alpha}
:

\displaystyle=\mathord{<_{\alpha^{\prime}}}\cap(N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)}\times N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)})

\displaystyle=\mathord{<_{\alpha^{\prime}}}\cap(N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha^{\prime},\psi)}\times N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha^{\prime},\psi)})

\displaystyle=\mathord{<_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha^{\prime},\psi)}}

Finally, for any
x\in N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)}
, we have:

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)}(x)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\lambda_{\alpha}(x)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\notin\mathsk{test}\\
\textsf{assume}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\in\mathsk{test}~\text{and}~\psi\Rightarrow x\\
\textsf{assume}~\lnot\lambda_{\alpha}(x)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\in\mathsk{test}~\text{and}~\psi\Rightarrow\lnot x\end{array}\right.

By virtue of the fact that
x\in N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)}
, we know that
\psi\Rightarrow\varphi_{\alpha}(x)
, and since
\psi\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}
, then
\psi\Rightarrow\lnot\mathsf{stuck}_{\alpha}
, therefore
\lambda_{\alpha}(x)
cannot be
\bot
, and since
\mathsk{label}
is a flat cpo, then it must be that
\lambda_{\alpha}(x)=\lambda_{\alpha^{\prime}}(x)
.

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\lambda_{\alpha^{\prime}}(x)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha^{\prime}}(x)\notin\mathsk{test}\\
\textsf{assume}~\lambda_{\alpha^{\prime}}(x)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha^{\prime}}(x)\in\mathsk{test}~\text{and}~\psi\Rightarrow x\\
\textsf{assume}~\lnot\lambda_{\alpha^{\prime}}(x)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha^{\prime}}(x)\in\mathsk{test}~\text{and}~\psi\Rightarrow\lnot x\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle=\lambda_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha^{\prime},\psi)}(x)

And finally, clearly we have
\varphi_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)}(x)=\varphi_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha^{\prime},\psi)}(x)=\mathsf{true}
.
∎

Lemma 73

(Monotonicity of
\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsf{fin}}
)
.

If
\boldsymbol{\alpha}\sqsubseteq_{{\mathsk{pom}}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}
, then
\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\boldsymbol{\alpha})\subseteq\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime})
.

Proof.

Since
\boldsymbol{\alpha}\sqsubseteq_{{\mathsk{pom}}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}
, then by
Lemma

33
there exists an
\alpha\in\boldsymbol{\alpha}
and
\alpha^{\prime}\in\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}
such that
\alpha\sqsubseteq_{\mathsk{lpof}}\alpha^{\prime}
. We now prove the claim as follows:

\displaystyle\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\boldsymbol{\alpha})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsf{fin}}([\alpha])

\displaystyle=\{[\beta]\mid\psi\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha},\beta\in\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha)\}

\displaystyle\subseteq\{[\beta]\mid\psi\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha^{\prime}},\beta\in\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha)\}

By
Lemma

51
.

\displaystyle=\{[\beta]\mid\psi\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha^{\prime}},\beta\in\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha^{\prime})\}

By
Lemma

72
.

\displaystyle=\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsf{fin}}([\alpha^{\prime}])

\displaystyle=\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime})

H.3
Translation and Pomset Operations

Lemma 74
.

For any
\ell\in\mathsk{act}\cup\{\mathsf{fork}\}
:

\mathsf{tr}(\boldsymbol{\langle}\ell\boldsymbol{\rangle})=\{\boldsymbol{\langle}\ell\boldsymbol{\rangle}\}

Proof.

Since
\boldsymbol{\langle}\ell\boldsymbol{\rangle}
is a finite pomset:

\displaystyle\mathsf{tr}(\boldsymbol{\langle}\ell\boldsymbol{\rangle})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\boldsymbol{\langle}\ell\boldsymbol{\rangle})

Fixing any arbitrary
x\in\mathsk{nodes}
, we have that
\boldsymbol{\langle}\ell\boldsymbol{\rangle}=[\boldsymbol{\langle}\ell\boldsymbol{\rangle}_{x}]
.

\displaystyle=\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsf{fin}}([\boldsymbol{\langle}\ell\boldsymbol{\rangle}_{x}])

\displaystyle=\{[\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\boldsymbol{\langle}\ell\boldsymbol{\rangle}_{x},\psi)]\mid\psi\in\mathsf{br}_{\boldsymbol{\langle}\ell\boldsymbol{\rangle}_{x}}\}

Since
\mathsf{br}_{\boldsymbol{\langle}\ell\boldsymbol{\rangle}_{x}}=\{\mathsf{true}\}
and
\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\boldsymbol{\langle}\ell\boldsymbol{\rangle}_{x},\mathsf{true})=\boldsymbol{\langle}\ell\boldsymbol{\rangle}_{x}
:

\displaystyle=\{[\boldsymbol{\langle}\ell\boldsymbol{\rangle}_{x}]\}

\displaystyle=\{\boldsymbol{\langle}\ell\boldsymbol{\rangle}\}\qed

For sequential composition of
lpof
s, where
\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}=\{\mathsf{true}\}
, we will write
\alpha\fatsemi\beta
(omitting the copy function
f
), to mean
\alpha\fatsemi_{f}\beta
where
f\triangleq[\mathsf{true}\mapsto\beta]
,
i.e.,

f
does not perform any renaming, since only a single isomorphic copy of
\beta
is needed.

Lemma 75
.

For any
\alpha,\beta\in{\mathsk{lpof}}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\mathsk{label})
,
f\in\mathsf{copy}_{\alpha,\beta}
,
\psi\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}
, and
\psi^{\prime}\in\mathsf{br}_{f(\psi)}
,

\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\fatsemi_{f}\beta,\psi\land\psi^{\prime})=\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)\fatsemi\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(f(\psi),\psi^{\prime})

Proof.

We show the equality component-wise. Some of the steps use
Lemma

71
. First, for the nodes, we have:

\displaystyle N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\fatsemi_{f}\beta,\psi\land\psi^{\prime})}

\displaystyle=\{x\in N_{\alpha\fatsemi_{f}\beta}\mid\psi\land\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\varphi_{\alpha\fatsemi_{f}\beta}(x)\}

Since
\psi\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}
, then we will only take nodes from
f(\psi)
and no other
\psi^{\prime\prime}\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\{x\in N_{\alpha}\mid\psi\land\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\varphi_{\alpha}(x)\}\cup\{x\in N_{f(\psi)}\mid\psi\land\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\varphi_{f(\psi)}(x)\land\psi\}

\displaystyle=\{x\in N_{\alpha}\mid\psi\Rightarrow\varphi_{\alpha}(x)\}\cup\{x\in N_{f(\psi)}\mid\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\varphi_{f(\psi)}(x)\}

\displaystyle=N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)}\cup N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(f(\psi),\psi^{\prime})}

\displaystyle=N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)\fatsemi\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(f(\psi),\psi^{\prime})}

Now, we show that the orders are the same:

\displaystyle\mathord{<_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\fatsemi_{f}\beta,\psi\land\psi^{\prime})}}

\displaystyle=\mathord{<_{\alpha\fatsemi_{f}\beta}}\cap(N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\fatsemi_{f}\beta,\psi\land\psi^{\prime})}\times N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\fatsemi_{f}\beta,\psi\land\psi^{\prime})})

\displaystyle=(\mathord{<_{\alpha}}\cap(N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)}\times N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)})\cup(\mathord{<_{f(\psi)}}\cap(N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(f(\psi),\psi^{\prime})}\times N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(f(\psi),\psi^{\prime})})\cup(N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)}\times N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(f(\psi),\psi^{\prime})})

\displaystyle=\mathord{<_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)\fatsemi\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(f(\psi),\psi^{\prime})}}

Now, for any
x
, clearly we have:

\varphi_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\fatsemi_{f}\beta,\psi\land\psi^{\prime})}(x)=\varphi_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)\fatsemi\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(f(\psi),\psi^{\prime})}(x)=\mathsf{true}

If
x\in N_{\alpha}
, then:

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\fatsemi_{f}\beta,\psi\land\psi^{\prime})}(x)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\lambda_{\alpha\fatsemi_{f}\beta}(x)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha\fatsemi_{f}\beta}(x)\notin\mathsk{test}\\
\textsf{assume}~\lambda_{\alpha\fatsemi_{f}\beta}(x)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha\fatsemi_{f}\beta}(x)\in\mathsk{test}~\text{and}~\psi\land\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow x\\
\textsf{assume}~\lnot\lambda_{\alpha\fatsemi_{f}\beta}(x)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha\fatsemi_{f}\beta}(x)\in\mathsk{test}~\text{and}~\psi\land\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\lnot x\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\lambda_{\alpha}(x)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\notin\mathsk{test}\\
\textsf{assume}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\in\mathsk{test}~\text{and}~\psi\Rightarrow x\\
\textsf{assume}~\lnot\lambda_{\alpha}(x)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\in\mathsk{test}~\text{and}~\psi\Rightarrow\lnot x\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle=\lambda_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)}(x)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)\fatsemi\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\psi^{\prime})}(x)

The case for
x\in f(\psi)
is nearly identical.
∎

Lemma 76
.

\mathsf{tr}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\fatsemi\boldsymbol{\beta})=\{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\fatsemi\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\mid\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\in\mathsf{tr}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}),\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\in\mathsf{tr}(\boldsymbol{\beta})\}
.

Proof.

\displaystyle\mathsf{tr}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\fatsemi\boldsymbol{\beta})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{tr}(\sup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\beta}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\fatsemi\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\fatsemi\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\{\alpha\fatsemi_{f}\beta\mid\alpha\in\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime},\beta\in\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime},f\in\mathsf{copy}_{\alpha,\beta}\})

Since the above set is an equivalence class, we can fix any
\alpha\in\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}
,
\beta\in\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}
, and
f\in\mathsf{copy}_{\alpha,\beta}
.

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsf{fin}}([\alpha\fatsemi_{f}\beta])

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\beta}}\{[\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\fatsemi_{f}\beta,\psi)]\mid\psi\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha\fatsemi_{f}\beta}\}

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\beta}}\{[\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\fatsemi_{f}\beta,\psi\land\psi^{\prime})]\mid\psi\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha},\psi^{\prime}\in\mathsf{br}_{f(\psi)}\}

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\beta}}\{[\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)\fatsemi\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(f(\psi),\psi^{\prime})]\mid\psi\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha},\psi^{\prime}\in\mathsf{br}_{f(\psi)}\}

(By
Lemma

75
)

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime})\fatsemi\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\left(\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime})\right)\fatsemi\left(\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime})\right)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{tr}(\boldsymbol{\alpha})\fatsemi\mathsf{tr}(\boldsymbol{\beta})

Lemma 77
.

\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta),\psi\land x)=\boldsymbol{\langle}\textsf{assume}~b\boldsymbol{\rangle}_{x}\fatsemi\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)

and

\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta),\psi\land\lnot x)=\boldsymbol{\langle}\textsf{assume}~\lnot b\boldsymbol{\rangle}_{x}\fatsemi\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\psi)
.

Proof.

We prove the equality for the first case. The second is entirely symmetrical. We first show equality of the node sets:

\displaystyle N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta),\psi\land x)}

\displaystyle=\{y\in N_{\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta)}\mid\psi\land x\Rightarrow\varphi_{\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta)}(y)\}

By the construction of guard,
x
is true in all nodes in the
\alpha
branch, and the root
x
has formula
\mathsf{true}
.

\displaystyle=\{x\}\cup\{y\in N_{\alpha}\mid\psi\Rightarrow\varphi_{\alpha}(y)\}

\displaystyle=N_{\boldsymbol{\langle}\textsf{assume}~b\boldsymbol{\rangle}_{x}}\cup N_{\mathsf{tr}(\alpha,\psi)}

\displaystyle=N_{\boldsymbol{\langle}\textsf{assume}~b\boldsymbol{\rangle}_{x}\fatsemi\mathsf{tr}(\alpha,\psi)}

For the order, we have:

\displaystyle\mathord{<_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta),\psi\land x)}}

\displaystyle=\mathord{<_{\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta)}}\cap(N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta),\psi\land x)}\times N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta),\psi\land x)})

\displaystyle=\left(\mathord{<_{\alpha}}\cup\mathord{<_{\beta}}\cup(\{x\}\times(N_{\alpha}\cup N_{\beta})\right)\cap(N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta),\psi\land x)}\times N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta),\psi\land x)})

Using what we just showed about the node sets:

\displaystyle=\left(\mathord{<_{\alpha}}\cap(N_{\mathsf{tr}(\alpha,\psi)}\times N_{\mathsf{tr}(\alpha,\psi)})\right)\cup(\{x\}\times N_{\mathsf{tr}(\alpha,\psi)})

\displaystyle=\mathord{<_{\mathsf{tr}(\alpha,\psi)}}\cup(\{x\}\times N_{\mathsf{tr}(\alpha,\psi)})

\displaystyle=<_{\boldsymbol{\langle}\textsf{assume}~b\boldsymbol{\rangle}_{x}\fatsemi\mathsf{tr}(\alpha,\psi)}

For every
y
, clearly:

\varphi_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta),\psi\land x)}(y)=\varphi_{\boldsymbol{\langle}\textsf{assume}~b\boldsymbol{\rangle}_{x}\fatsemi\mathsf{tr}(\alpha,\psi)}(y)=\mathsf{true}

If
y=x
, then we have:

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta),\psi\land x)}(y)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\lambda_{\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta)}(y)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta)}(y)\notin\mathsk{test}\\
\textsf{assume}~\lambda_{\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta)}(y)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta)}(y)\in\mathsk{test}~\text{and}~\psi\land x\Rightarrow y\\
\textsf{assume}~\lnot\lambda_{\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta)}(y)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta)}(y)\in\mathsk{test}~\text{and}~\psi\land x\Rightarrow\lnot y\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle=\textsf{assume}~\lambda_{\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta)}(x)

\displaystyle=\textsf{assume}~b

\displaystyle=\lambda_{\boldsymbol{\langle}\textsf{assume}~b\boldsymbol{\rangle}_{x}}(x)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{\boldsymbol{\langle}\textsf{assume}~b\boldsymbol{\rangle}_{x}\fatsemi\mathsf{tr}(\alpha,\psi)}(x)

If not, then
y\in\mathsf{tr}(\alpha,\psi)\subseteq N_{\alpha}
:

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta),\psi\land x)}(y)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\lambda_{\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta)}(y)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta)}(y)\notin\mathsk{test}\\
\textsf{assume}~\lambda_{\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta)}(y)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta)}(y)\in\mathsk{test}~\text{and}~\psi\land x\Rightarrow y\\
\textsf{assume}~\lnot\lambda_{\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta)}(y)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta)}(y)\in\mathsk{test}~\text{and}~\psi\land x\Rightarrow\lnot y\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\lambda_{\alpha}(y)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(y)\notin\mathsk{test}\\
\textsf{assume}~\lambda_{\alpha}(y)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(y)\in\mathsk{test}~\text{and}~\psi\Rightarrow y\\
\textsf{assume}~\lnot\lambda_{\alpha}(y)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(y)\in\mathsk{test}~\text{and}~\psi\Rightarrow\lnot y\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle=\lambda_{\mathsf{tr}(\alpha,\psi)}(y)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{\boldsymbol{\langle}\textsf{assume}~b\boldsymbol{\rangle}_{x}\fatsemi\mathsf{tr}(\alpha,\psi)}(y)\qed

Lemma 78
.

\mathsf{tr}(\mathsf{guard}(b,\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}))=\{\boldsymbol{\langle}\textsf{assume}~b\boldsymbol{\rangle}\fatsemi\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\mid\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\in\mathsf{tr}(\boldsymbol{\alpha})\}\cup\{\boldsymbol{\langle}\textsf{assume}~\lnot b\boldsymbol{\rangle}\fatsemi\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\in\mathsf{tr}(\boldsymbol{\beta})\}

Proof.

\displaystyle\mathsf{tr}(\mathsf{guard}(b,\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}))

\displaystyle=\mathsf{tr}\left(\sup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathsf{guard}(b,\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime})\right)

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\mathsf{guard}(b,\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\{\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta)\mid\alpha\in\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime},\beta\in\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime},x\in\mathsk{nodes}\})

Since the set above is an equivalence class, we can fix any
x
,
\alpha
, and
\beta
and write:

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsf{fin}}([\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta)])

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\beta}}\{[\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta),\psi)]\mid\psi\in\mathsf{br}_{\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta)}\}

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\beta}}\{[\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta),\psi\land x)]\mid\psi\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}\}\cup\{[\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\mathsf{guard}(x,b,\alpha,\beta),\psi\land\lnot x)]\mid\psi\in\mathsf{br}_{\beta}\}

By
Lemma

77
:

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\beta}}\{[\boldsymbol{\langle}\textsf{assume}~b\boldsymbol{\rangle}_{x}\fatsemi\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)]\mid\psi\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}\}\cup\{[\boldsymbol{\langle}\textsf{assume}~\lnot b\boldsymbol{\rangle}_{x}\fatsemi\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\psi)]\mid\psi\in\mathsf{br}_{\beta}\}

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\beta}}\{\boldsymbol{\langle}\textsf{assume}~b\boldsymbol{\rangle}\fatsemi\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime\prime}\mid\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime})\}\cup\{\boldsymbol{\langle}\textsf{assume}~\lnot b\boldsymbol{\rangle}\fatsemi\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime\prime}\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime})\}

\displaystyle=\{\boldsymbol{\langle}\textsf{assume}~b\boldsymbol{\rangle}\fatsemi\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime\prime}\mid\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathsf{tr}(\boldsymbol{\alpha})\}\cup\{\boldsymbol{\langle}\textsf{assume}~\lnot b\boldsymbol{\rangle}\fatsemi\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime\prime}\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathsf{tr}(\boldsymbol{\beta})\}\qed

Lemma 79
.

For any
\alpha,\beta\in{\mathsk{lpof}}_{\mathsf{fin}}
,
\psi\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}
, and
\psi^{\prime}\in\mathsf{br}_{\beta}
:

\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\parallel_{x}\beta,\psi\land\psi^{\prime})=\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)\parallel_{x}\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\psi^{\prime})

Proof.

We show the equality for each component. First, for the nodes, we have:

\displaystyle N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\parallel_{x}\beta,\psi\land\psi^{\prime})}

\displaystyle=\{y\in N_{\alpha\parallel_{x}\beta}\mid\psi\land\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\varphi_{\alpha\parallel_{x}\beta}(y)\}

\displaystyle=\{x\}\cup\{y\in\mathsf{nFork}_{\alpha}\mid\psi\Rightarrow\varphi_{\alpha}(y)\}\cup\{z\in\mathsf{nFork}_{\beta}\mid\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\varphi_{\beta}(z)\}

\displaystyle=\{x\}\cup\mathsf{nFork}_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)}\cup\mathsf{nFork}_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\psi^{\prime})}

\displaystyle=N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)\parallel_{x}\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\psi^{\prime})}

Now, for the orders:

\displaystyle\mathord{<_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\parallel_{x}\beta,\psi\land\psi^{\prime})}}

\displaystyle=\mathord{<_{\alpha\parallel_{x}\beta}}\cap\left(N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\parallel_{x}\beta,\psi\land\psi^{\prime})}\times N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\parallel_{x}\beta,\psi\land\psi^{\prime})}\right)

\displaystyle=\left((\{x\}\times(\mathsf{nFork}_{\alpha}\cup\mathsf{nFork}_{\beta}))\cup\ (\mathord{<_{\alpha}}\cap(\mathsf{nFork}_{\alpha}\times\mathsf{nFork}_{\alpha}))\cup\ (\mathord{<_{\beta}}\cap(\mathsf{nFork}_{\beta}\times\mathsf{nFork}_{\beta}))\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\cap\left(N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\parallel_{x}\beta,\psi\land\psi^{\prime})}\times N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\parallel_{x}\beta,\psi\land\psi^{\prime})}\right)

\displaystyle=(\{x\}\times(\mathsf{nFork}_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)}\cup\mathsf{nFork}_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\psi^{\prime})}))

\displaystyle\quad\cup\ (\mathord{<_{\alpha}}\cap(\mathsf{nFork}_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)}\times\mathsf{nFork}_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)}))

\displaystyle\quad\cup\ (\mathord{<_{\beta}}\cap(\mathsf{nFork}_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\psi^{\prime})}\times\mathsf{nFork}_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\psi^{\prime})})

\displaystyle=\mathord{<_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)\parallel_{x}\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\psi^{\prime})}}

Now take any
y
, clearly we have:

\varphi_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\parallel_{x}\beta,\psi\land\psi^{\prime})}(y)=\varphi_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)\parallel_{x}\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\psi^{\prime})}(y)=\mathsf{true}

Now, if
y=x
, then
\lambda_{\alpha\parallel_{x}\beta}(y)=\mathsf{fork}
, so:

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\parallel_{x}\beta,\psi\land\psi^{\prime})}(y)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\lambda_{\alpha\parallel_{x}\beta}(y)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha\parallel_{x}\beta}(y)\notin\mathsk{test}\\
\textsf{assume}~\lambda_{\alpha\parallel_{x}\beta}(y)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha\parallel_{x}\beta}(y)\in\mathsk{test}~\text{and}~\psi\land\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow x\\
\textsf{assume}~\lnot\lambda_{\alpha\parallel_{x}\beta}(y)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha\parallel_{x}\beta}(y)\in\mathsk{test}~\text{and}~\psi\land\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\lnot x\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle=\lambda_{\alpha\parallel_{x}\beta}(y)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{fork}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)\parallel_{x}\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\psi^{\prime})}(y)

Now, if
y\in\mathsf{nFork}_{\alpha}
, then:

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\parallel_{x}\beta,\psi\land\psi^{\prime})}(y)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\lambda_{\alpha\parallel_{x}\beta}(y)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha\parallel_{x}\beta}(y)\notin\mathsk{test}\\
\textsf{assume}~\lambda_{\alpha\parallel_{x}\beta}(y)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha\parallel_{x}\beta}(y)\in\mathsk{test}~\text{and}~\psi\land\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow x\\
\textsf{assume}~\lnot\lambda_{\alpha\parallel_{x}\beta}(y)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha\parallel_{x}\beta}(y)\in\mathsk{test}~\text{and}~\psi\land\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\lnot x\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\lambda_{\alpha}(y)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(y)\notin\mathsk{test}\\
\textsf{assume}~\lambda_{\alpha}(y)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(y)\in\mathsk{test}~\text{and}~\psi\Rightarrow x\\
\textsf{assume}~\lnot\lambda_{\alpha}(y)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(y)\in\mathsk{test}~\text{and}~\psi\Rightarrow\lnot x\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle=\lambda_{\mathsf{tr}(\alpha,\psi)}(y)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)\parallel_{x}\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\psi^{\prime})}(y)

The case where
y\in\mathsf{nBot}(\beta)
is nearly identical.
∎

Since
\parallel
is not monotone (see
Remark

12
), we need a different way to approximate infinite pomsets composed in parallel, which we now describe. Let
\mathsf{root}(\boldsymbol{\gamma})
be the label of the root node of the pomset
\boldsymbol{\gamma}
and

\boldsymbol{\gamma}\ll_{1}\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{\prime}\qquad\text{iff}\qquad\boldsymbol{\gamma}\ll\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{\prime}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathsf{root}(\boldsymbol{\gamma})=\mathsf{root}(\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{\prime})=\mathsf{fork}~\text{or}~\mathsf{root}(\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{\prime})\neq\mathsf{fork}

i.e.,

\boldsymbol{\gamma}\ll_{1}\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{\prime}
is similar to the standard approximation order
\ll
on pomsets, but it prevents a fork node at the root of
\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{\prime}
from being converted to
\bot
, which would cause the root of a parallel composition involving
\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{\prime}
vs
\boldsymbol{\gamma}
to have a different number of successors.
Coming back to the example of
Remark

12
, we have that
[\alpha]\sqsubseteq_{{\mathsk{pom}}}[\beta]
and also
[\alpha]\ll[\beta]
, whereas
[\alpha]\not\ll_{1}[\beta]
, since the root of
\beta
is
\mathsf{fork}
but not so is the root of
\alpha
.

We need two properties of
\ll_{1}
. First:

\sup_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}\ll_{1}\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{\gamma}=\sup_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}\ll\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{\gamma}

(7)

By
Lemma

9
and
Corollary

47
, we have that
\sup_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}\ll\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{\gamma}=\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{\prime}
. If
\mathsf{root}(\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{\prime})=\mathsf{fork}
, then
\{\boldsymbol{\gamma}\mid\boldsymbol{\gamma}\ll_{1}\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{\prime}\}=\{\boldsymbol{\gamma}\mid\boldsymbol{\gamma}\ll\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{\prime}\}\setminus\{\bot_{\mathsk{pom}}\}
, which clearly has the same supremum, as we have only removed the bottom element from the set. If
\mathsf{root}(\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{\prime})\neq\mathsf{fork}
, then the orders are equivalent.
The second property that we need is

{\lfloor\boldsymbol{\alpha}\parallel\boldsymbol{\beta}\rfloor}_{\mathsf{fin}}\setminus\{\bot_{\mathsk{pom}}\}=\{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\parallel\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\mid\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\ll_{1}\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\ll_{1}\boldsymbol{\beta}\}

(8)

This can be easily seen by observing that
\bot_{\mathsk{pom}}
is not included in the right set, since all parallel compositions have fork at the root therein. Moreover, the successors nodes (but not their labels) of the root are also fixed in both cases: on the left they are fixed because the root is fixed, and on the right they are fixed due to the
\ll_{1}
order, since any
\mathsf{fork}
node at the root would be removed by the parallel composition. Above level 2, both sets allow for any approximation of
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
and
\boldsymbol{\beta}
.

Lemma 80
.

\mathsf{tr}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\parallel\boldsymbol{\beta})=\{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\parallel\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\mid\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\in\mathsf{tr}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}),\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\in\mathsf{tr}(\boldsymbol{\beta})\}
.

Proof.

\displaystyle\mathsf{tr}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}\parallel\boldsymbol{\beta})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{tr}\left(\sup_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}\in{\lfloor\boldsymbol{\alpha}\parallel\boldsymbol{\beta}\rfloor}_{\mathsf{fin}}}\boldsymbol{\gamma}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{tr}(\boldsymbol{\langle}\bot\boldsymbol{\rangle})\cup\mathsf{tr}\left(\sup_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}\in{\lfloor\boldsymbol{\alpha}\parallel\boldsymbol{\beta}\rfloor}_{\mathsf{fin}}\setminus\{\bot_{\mathsk{pom}}\}}\boldsymbol{\gamma}\right)

By
Equation

8
.

\displaystyle=\emptyset\cup\mathsf{tr}\left(\sup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\ll_{1}\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\sup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\ll_{1}\boldsymbol{\beta}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\parallel\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\ll_{1}\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\ll_{1}\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\parallel\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\ll_{1}\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\ll_{1}\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\{\alpha\parallel_{x}\beta\mid\alpha\in\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime},\beta\in\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime},x\in\mathsk{nodes}\})

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\ll_{1}\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\ll_{1}\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsf{fin}}([\alpha\parallel_{x}\beta])

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\ll_{1}\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\ll_{1}\boldsymbol{\beta}}\{[\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\parallel_{x}\beta,\psi)]\mid\psi\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha\parallel_{x}\beta}\}

By
Lemma

79
.

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\ll_{1}\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\ll_{1}\boldsymbol{\beta}}\{[\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha\parallel_{x}\beta,\psi\land\psi^{\prime})]\mid\psi\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha},\psi^{\prime}\in\mathsf{br}_{\beta}\}

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\ll_{1}\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\ll_{1}\boldsymbol{\beta}}\{[\{\alpha^{\prime}\parallel_{x}\beta^{\prime}\mid\alpha^{\prime}\in\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi),\beta^{\prime}\in\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\psi^{\prime})\}]\mid\psi\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha},\psi^{\prime}\in\mathsf{br}_{\beta}\}

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\ll_{1}\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\ll_{1}\boldsymbol{\beta}}\{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime\prime}\parallel\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime\prime}\mid\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime\prime}\in\{[\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)]\mid\psi\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}\},\ \boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime\prime}\in\{[\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\beta,\psi^{\prime})]\mid\psi^{\prime}\in\mathsf{br}_{\beta}\}\}

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\ll_{1}\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}\ll_{1}\boldsymbol{\beta}}\{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime\prime}\parallel\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime\prime}\mid\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}),\ \boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime})\}

By
Equation

7
.

\displaystyle=\{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime\prime}\parallel\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime\prime}\mid\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathsf{tr}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}),\ \boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathsf{tr}(\boldsymbol{\beta})\}

Lemma 81
.

If
\mathsf{tr}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)=\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{PL}}
, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
:

\mathsf{tr}(\Phi_{\langle C,b\rangle}^{n}(\bot_{\mathsk{pom}}))=\Xi_{\langle C,b\rangle}^{n}(\emptyset)

where above we use
\bot_{\mathsk{pom}}=\boldsymbol{\langle}\bot\boldsymbol{\rangle}
to disambiguate.

Proof.

The proof is by induction on
n
. If
n=0
, then we have:

\mathsf{tr}\left(\Phi_{\langle C,b\rangle}^{0}(\bot_{\mathsk{pom}})\right)=\mathsf{tr}\left(\bot_{\mathsk{pom}}\right)=\{[\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\boldsymbol{\langle}\bot\boldsymbol{\rangle}_{x},\psi)]\mid\psi\in\mathsf{br}_{\boldsymbol{\langle}\bot\boldsymbol{\rangle}_{x}}\}=\emptyset=\Xi_{\langle C,b\rangle}^{0}(\emptyset)

Where the second to last step follows from the fact that
\mathsf{br}_{\boldsymbol{\langle}\bot\boldsymbol{\rangle}_{x}}=\emptyset
. Now, suppose the claim holds for
n
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathsf{tr}(\Phi_{\langle C,b\rangle}^{n+1}(\bot_{\mathsk{pom}}))

\displaystyle=\mathsf{tr}\left(\Phi_{\langle C,b\rangle}\left(\Phi_{\langle C,b\rangle}^{n}(\bot_{\mathsk{pom}})\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{tr}\left(\mathsf{guard}\left(b,\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket\fatsemi\Phi_{\langle C,b\rangle}^{n}(\emptyset),\left\llbracket\textsf{skip}\right\rrbracket\right)\right)

By
Lemma

78
.

\displaystyle=\left\{\boldsymbol{\langle}\textsf{assume}~b\boldsymbol{\rangle}\fatsemi\boldsymbol{\alpha}\mid\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathsf{tr}\left(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket\fatsemi\Phi_{\langle C,b\rangle}^{n}(\emptyset)\right)\right\}\cup\left\{\boldsymbol{\langle}\textsf{assume}~b\boldsymbol{\rangle}\fatsemi\boldsymbol{\beta}\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathsf{tr}(\left\llbracket\textsf{skip}\right\rrbracket)\right\}

By
Lemmas

74
and
76
.

\displaystyle=\left\{\boldsymbol{\langle}\textsf{assume}~b\boldsymbol{\rangle}\fatsemi\boldsymbol{\alpha}\fatsemi\boldsymbol{\beta}\mid\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathsf{tr}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket),\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathsf{tr}\left(\Phi_{\langle C,b\rangle}^{n}(\emptyset)\right)\right\}\cup\left\{\boldsymbol{\langle}\textsf{assume}~b\boldsymbol{\rangle}\fatsemi\left\llbracket\textsf{skip}\right\rrbracket\right\}

By assumption, and the induction hypothesis:

\displaystyle=\left\{\boldsymbol{\langle}\textsf{assume}~b\boldsymbol{\rangle}\fatsemi\boldsymbol{\alpha}\fatsemi\boldsymbol{\beta}\mid\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{PL}},\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\Xi_{\langle C,b\rangle}^{n}(\emptyset)\right\}\cup\left\{\boldsymbol{\langle}\textsf{assume}~b\boldsymbol{\rangle}\fatsemi\left\llbracket\textsf{skip}\right\rrbracket\right\}

\displaystyle=\Xi_{\langle C,b\rangle}\left(\Xi_{\langle C,b\rangle}^{n}(\emptyset)\right)

\displaystyle=\Xi_{\langle C,b\rangle}^{n+1}(\emptyset)\qed

H.4
Translations and Program Semantics

Lemma 82
.

For any program
C\in\mathsk{cmd}
,
\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{PL}}=\mathsf{tr}(\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket)
.

Proof.

The proof is by induction on the structure of the program.

•

C=\textsf{skip}
or
C\in\mathsk{act}
. Follows immediately from
Lemma

74
.

•

C=C_{1};C_{2}
.

\displaystyle\left\llbracket C_{1};C_{2}\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{PL}}

\displaystyle=\{\boldsymbol{\alpha}\fatsemi\boldsymbol{\beta}\mid\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{PL}},\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{PL}}\}

\displaystyle=\{\boldsymbol{\alpha}\fatsemi\boldsymbol{\beta}\mid\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathsf{tr}(\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket),\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathsf{tr}(\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket)\}

(By the induction hypothesis.)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{tr}(\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket\fatsemi\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket)

(By
Lemma

76
.)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{tr}(\left\llbracket C_{1};C_{2}\right\rrbracket)

•

C=C_{1}\,|\,C_{2}
.

\displaystyle\left\llbracket C_{1}\,|\,C_{2}\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{PL}}

\displaystyle=\{\boldsymbol{\alpha}\parallel\boldsymbol{\beta}\mid\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{PL}},\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{PL}}\}

\displaystyle=\{\boldsymbol{\alpha}\parallel\boldsymbol{\beta}\mid\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathsf{tr}(\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket),\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathsf{tr}(\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket)\}

(By the induction hypothesis.)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{tr}(\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket\parallel\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket)

(By
Lemma

80
.)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{tr}(\left\llbracket C_{1}\,|\,C_{2}\right\rrbracket)

•

C=\textsf{if}~b~\textsf{then}~C_{1}~\textsf{else}~C_{2}
.

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\textsf{if}~b~\textsf{then}~C_{1}~\textsf{else}~C_{2}\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{PL}}

\displaystyle=\{\boldsymbol{\langle}\textsf{assume}~b\boldsymbol{\rangle}\fatsemi\boldsymbol{\alpha}\mid\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{PL}}\}\cup\{\boldsymbol{\langle}\textsf{assume}~\lnot b\boldsymbol{\rangle}\fatsemi\boldsymbol{\beta}\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{PL}}\}

By the induction hypothesis:

\displaystyle=\{\boldsymbol{\langle}\textsf{assume}~b\boldsymbol{\rangle}\fatsemi\boldsymbol{\alpha}\mid\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathsf{tr}(\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket)\}\cup\{\boldsymbol{\langle}\textsf{assume}~\lnot b\boldsymbol{\rangle}\fatsemi\boldsymbol{\beta}\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathsf{tr}(\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket)\}

By
Lemma

78
.

\displaystyle=\mathsf{tr}(\mathsf{guard}(b,\left\llbracket C_{1}\right\rrbracket,\left\llbracket C_{2}\right\rrbracket))

\displaystyle=\mathsf{tr}(\left\llbracket\textsf{if}~b~\textsf{then}~C_{1}~\textsf{else}~C_{2}\right\rrbracket)

•

C=\textsf{while}~b~\textsf{do}~C^{\prime}
.

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\textsf{while}~b~\textsf{do}~C^{\prime}\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{PL}}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{lfp}\left(\Xi_{\langle C^{\prime},b\rangle}\right)

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\Xi_{\langle C^{\prime},b\rangle}^{n}(\emptyset)

(By the Kleene fixed point theorem.)

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathsf{tr}\left(\Phi_{\langle C^{\prime},b\rangle}^{n}(\bot_{\mathsk{pom}})\right)

(By
Lemma

81
, and the induction hypothesis.)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{tr}\left(\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\Phi_{\langle C^{\prime},b\rangle}^{n}(\bot_{\mathsk{pom}})\right)

(By Scott continuity of
\mathsf{tr}
.)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{tr}\left(\mathsf{lfp}\left(\Phi_{\langle C^{\prime},b\rangle}^{n}\right)\right)

(By the Kleene fixed point theorem.)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{tr}\left(\left\llbracket\textsf{while}~b~\textsf{do}~C^{\prime}\right\rrbracket\right)

H.5
Translations and Linearization

Lemma 83
.

For any binary branching
\alpha\in{\mathsk{lpof}}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\mathsk{label})
,
S\subseteq\mathsf{nBot}_{\alpha}
, and
\psi\in\mathsf{valid}_{\alpha}(S)
, then:

\bigcup_{\psi^{\prime}\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}:\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\psi}(\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi^{\prime}),\mathsf{true},S)=(\alpha,\psi,S)\setminus\{x\in N_{\alpha}\mid\varphi_{\alpha}(x)\Rightarrow\mathsf{stuck}_{\alpha}\}

Proof.

We show the inclusion in both directions.

Take any
x\in\bigcup_{\psi^{\prime}\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}:\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\psi}(\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi^{\prime}),\mathsf{true},S)
, so there is a
\psi^{\prime}\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}
such that
\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\psi
and
x\in N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi^{\prime})}\setminus S
such that
{x\mathclose{\downarrow}}_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi^{\prime})}\subseteq S
. From
x\in N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi^{\prime})}\setminus S
, we get that
x\in N_{\alpha}\setminus S
and
\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\varphi_{\alpha}(x)
. We now show that
{x\mathclose{\downarrow}}_{\alpha}\subseteq S
. Take any
y<_{\alpha}x
, so we know that
\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\varphi_{\alpha}(x)\Rightarrow\varphi_{\alpha}(y)
, which means that
y\in N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi^{\prime})}
, and therefore
y<_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi^{\prime})}x
, so it must be that
y\in S
.
From
\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\psi
and
\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\varphi_{\alpha}(x)
, we know that
\varphi_{\alpha}(x)\land\psi
is satisfiable. Since
\psi\in\mathsf{valid}_{\alpha}(S)
, then
\psi
must assign a truth value to every test in
S
, and since we just showed that
{x\mathclose{\downarrow}}_{\alpha}\subseteq S
, then
\mathsf{vars}(\varphi_{\alpha}(x))\subseteq S
, and so we must have that
\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\varphi_{\alpha}(x)
. Therefore, we have established all the conditions of
x\in(\alpha,\psi,S)
. Clearly also
\varphi_{\alpha}(x)\not\Rightarrow\mathsf{stuck}_{\alpha}
since
\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\varphi_{\alpha}(x)
and
\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\lnot\mathsf{stuck}_{\alpha}
.

Now take any
x\in(\alpha,\psi,S)\setminus\{x\in N_{\alpha}\mid\varphi_{\alpha}(x)\Rightarrow\mathsf{stuck}_{\alpha}\}
, so
x\in N_{\alpha}\setminus S
,
\psi\Rightarrow\varphi_{\alpha}(x)
,
{x\mathclose{\downarrow}}_{\alpha}\subseteq S
, and
\varphi_{\alpha}(x)\not\Rightarrow\mathsf{stuck}_{\alpha}
. Since
\varphi_{\alpha}(x)\not\Rightarrow\mathsf{stuck}_{\alpha}
, then
x\in\mathsf{ext}_{\alpha}
, and so there is clearly a
\psi^{\prime}\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}
such that
\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\varphi_{\alpha}(x)
. This means that
x\in N_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi^{\prime})}\setminus S
. Since by definition
\varphi_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi^{\prime})}(x)=\mathsf{true}
, then clearly
\mathsf{true}\Rightarrow\varphi_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi^{\prime})}(x)
. Finally, we will show that
{x\mathclose{\downarrow}}_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi^{\prime})}\subseteq S
. Take any
y<_{\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi^{\prime})}x
, this means that
y<_{\alpha}x
, so clearly
y\in S
. Therefore, we have that
x\in(\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi^{\prime}),\mathsf{true},S)\subseteq\bigcup_{\psi^{\prime}\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}:\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\psi}(\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi^{\prime}),\mathsf{true},S)
.
∎

Lemma 84
.

If
S\subseteq\mathsf{nBot}_{\alpha}
,
\psi\in\mathsf{valid}_{\alpha}(S)
and
\psi\Rightarrow\mathsf{stuck}_{\alpha}
, then
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi,S)=\emptyset
.

Proof.

The proof is by induction on the size of the set
{}^{*}(\alpha,\psi,S)
. If
{}^{*}(\alpha,\psi,S)=\emptyset
, then by
Lemma

63
we know that
\psi\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}
, but this is impossible since
\psi\Rightarrow\mathsf{stuck}_{\alpha}
. Therefore, it cannot be the case that
{}^{*}(\alpha,\psi,S)=\emptyset
.

Now suppose that
{}^{*}(\alpha,\psi,S)\neq\emptyset
. By
Lemma

64
, we know that
(\alpha,\psi,S)\neq\emptyset
. So, we have:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi,S)

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{x\in(\alpha,\psi,S)}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi,S\cup\{x\})^{\dagger}(\left\llbracket\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\right\rrbracket{act}(s))&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\in\mathsk{act}\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi\land\llparenthesis x={\left\llbracket\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\right\rrbracket}{test}(s)\rrparenthesis,S\cup\{x\})(s)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\in\mathsk{test}\\
\emptyset&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)=\bot\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi,S\cup\{x\})(s)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)=\mathsf{fork}\end{array}\right.

By the induction hypothesis in the first, second, and fourth cases, we get:

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{x\in(\alpha,\psi,S)}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\emptyset&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\in\mathsk{act}\\
\emptyset&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\in\mathsk{test}\\
\emptyset&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)=\bot\\
\emptyset&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)=\mathsf{fork}\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{x\in(\alpha,\psi,S)}\emptyset=\emptyset\qed

Lemma 85
.

For any binary branching
\alpha\in{\mathsk{lpof}}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\mathsk{label})
,
S\subseteq\mathsf{nBot}_{\alpha}
, and
\psi\in\mathsf{valid}_{\alpha}(S)
:

\bigcup_{\psi^{\prime}\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}:\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\psi}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi^{\prime}),\mathsf{true},S)(s)=\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi,S)(s)

Proof.

The proof is by induction on the size of the set
{}^{*}(\alpha,\psi,S)
. If
{}^{*}(\alpha,\psi,S)=\emptyset
, then clearly
(\alpha,\psi,S)=\emptyset
and by
Lemma

63
, we know that
\psi\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}
. That means that the union on the left side is only over a single term, generated from
\psi
. Now,
\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi)
contains all those nodes
x
such that
\psi\Rightarrow\varphi_{\alpha}(x)
, but we know from
{}^{*}(\alpha,\psi,S)=\emptyset
that all such nodes are already in
S
, therefore by
Lemma

83
,
(\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi),\mathsf{true},S)=\emptyset
too. This gives us

\bigcup_{\psi^{\prime}\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}:\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\psi}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi^{\prime}),\mathsf{true},S)(s)=\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi),\mathsf{true},S)(s)=\{s\}=\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi,S)(s)

Now, assume that
{}^{*}(\alpha,\psi,S)\neq\emptyset
, so by
Lemma

64
, then
(\alpha,\psi,S)\neq\emptyset
.

Suppose that
\varphi_{\alpha}(x)\Rightarrow\mathsf{stuck}_{\alpha}
for some
x\in(\alpha,\psi,S)
. This means that
\psi\Rightarrow\varphi_{\alpha}(x)\Rightarrow\mathsf{stuck}_{\alpha}
, so clearly there can be no
\psi\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}
such that
\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\psi
, so
\bigcup_{\psi^{\prime}\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}:\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\psi}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi^{\prime}),\mathsf{true},S)(s)=\emptyset
. In addition, by
Lemma

84
,
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi,S)(s)=\emptyset
too.

So, we are left with the case where
\varphi_{\alpha}(x)\not\Rightarrow\mathsf{stuck}_{\alpha}
for all
x\in(\alpha,\psi,S)
. Let
\alpha_{\psi^{\prime}}=\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi^{\prime})
. We now have:

\displaystyle\bigcup_{\psi^{\prime}\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}:\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\psi}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha_{\psi^{\prime}},\mathsf{true},S)

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\psi^{\prime}\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}:\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\psi}\bigcup_{x\in(\alpha_{\psi^{\prime}},\mathsf{true},S)}\left\{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha_{\psi^{\prime}},\mathsf{true},S\cup\{x\})^{\dagger}(\left\llbracket\lambda_{\alpha_{\psi^{\prime}}}(x)\right\rrbracket{act}(s))&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha_{\psi^{\prime}}}(x)\in\mathsk{act}_{\mathsf{PL}}\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha_{\psi^{\prime}},\mathsf{true}\land\llparenthesis x={\left\llbracket\lambda_{\alpha_{\psi^{\prime}}}(x)\right\rrbracket}{test}(s)\rrparenthesis,S\cup\{x\})(s)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha_{\psi^{\prime}}}(x)\in\mathsk{test}_{\mathsf{PL}}\\
\emptyset&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha_{\psi^{\prime}}}(x)=\bot\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha_{\psi^{\prime}},\mathsf{true},S\cup\{x\})(s)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha_{\psi^{\prime}}}(x)=\mathsf{fork}\end{array}\right.

Since
\alpha_{\psi^{\prime}}
has no test or
\bot
nodes, we can remove those two cases. In addition, we expand the case for actions into cases for whether or not the action is assume.

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\psi^{\prime}\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}:\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\psi}\bigcup_{x\in(\alpha_{\psi^{\prime}},\mathsf{true},S)}\left\{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha_{\psi^{\prime}},\mathsf{true},S\cup\{x\})^{\dagger}(\left\llbracket\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\right\rrbracket{act}(s))&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\in\mathsk{act}\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha_{\psi^{\prime}},\mathsf{true},S\cup\{x\})^{\dagger}(\left\llbracket\textsf{assume}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\right\rrbracket{act}(s))&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\in\mathsk{test}~\text{and}~\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow x\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha_{\psi^{\prime}},\mathsf{true},S\cup\{x\})^{\dagger}(\left\llbracket\textsf{assume}~\lnot\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\right\rrbracket{act}(s))&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\in\mathsk{test}~\text{and}~\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\lnot x\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha_{\psi^{\prime}},\mathsf{true},S\cup\{x\})(s)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)=\mathsf{fork}\end{array}\right.

By
Lemma

83
, and the fact that
\psi\Rightarrow\varphi_{\alpha}(x)
for all
x\in(\alpha,\psi,S)
, we can rearrange the unions and move the inner one inside of the conditional.

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{x\in(\alpha,\psi,S)}\left\{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{ll}\bigcup_{\psi^{\prime}\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}:\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\psi}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha_{\psi^{\prime}},\mathsf{true},S\cup\{x\})^{\dagger}(\left\llbracket\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\right\rrbracket{act}(s))&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\in\mathsk{act}\\
\bigcup_{\psi^{\prime}\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}:\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\psi\land x}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha_{\psi^{\prime}},\mathsf{true},S\cup\{x\})^{\dagger}(\left\llbracket\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\right\rrbracket{test}(s))&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\in\mathsk{test}\\
\hskip 8.50012pt\cup\bigcup_{\psi^{\prime}\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}:\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\psi\land\lnot x}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha_{\psi^{\prime}},\mathsf{true},S\cup\{x\})^{\dagger}(\left\llbracket\lnot\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\right\rrbracket{test}(s))\\
\bigcup_{\psi^{\prime}\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}:\psi^{\prime}\Rightarrow\psi}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha_{\psi^{\prime}},\mathsf{true},S\cup\{x\})(s)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)=\mathsf{fork}\end{array}\right.

By the induction hypothesis.

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{x\in(\alpha,\psi,S)}\left\{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi,S\cup\{x\})^{\dagger}(\left\llbracket\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\right\rrbracket{act}(s))&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\in\mathsk{act}\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi\land x,S\cup\{x\})^{\dagger}(\left\llbracket\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\right\rrbracket{test}(s))&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\in\mathsk{test}\\
\hskip 8.50012pt\cup\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi\land\lnot x,S\cup\{x\})^{\dagger}(\left\llbracket\lnot\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\right\rrbracket{test}(s))\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi,S\cup\{x\})(s)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)=\mathsf{fork}\end{array}\right.

Exactly one of
\left\llbracket\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\right\rrbracket{test}(s)
and
\left\llbracket\lnot\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\right\rrbracket{test}(s)
will evaluate to
\{s\}
and the other will evaluate to
\emptyset
, so we can consolidate the terms.

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{x\in(\alpha,\psi,S)}\left\{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi,S\cup\{x\})^{\dagger}(\left\llbracket\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\right\rrbracket{act}(s))&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\in\mathsk{act}\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi\land\llparenthesis x=\left\llbracket\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\right\rrbracket{test}(s)\rrparenthesis,S\cup\{x\})(s)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\in\mathsk{test}\\
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi,S\cup\{x\})(s)&\text{if}~\lambda_{\alpha}(x)=\mathsf{fork}\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi,S)\qed

Lemma 86
.

For any
\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in{\mathsk{pom}}
and
s\in S
,
\mathcal{L}(\boldsymbol{\alpha})(s)=\mathcal{L}_{\mathsf{PL}}(\mathsf{tr}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}))(s)
.

Proof.

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\boldsymbol{\alpha})(s)

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime})(s)

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{[\alpha]\ll\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\mathsf{true},\emptyset)(s)

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{[\alpha]\ll\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bigcup_{\psi\in\mathsf{br}_{\alpha}}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsk{lpof}}(\alpha,\psi),\mathsf{true},\emptyset)(s)

By
Lemma

85

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime})}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\boldsymbol{\beta})(s)

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime}\ll\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\mathsf{tr}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\prime})}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\boldsymbol{\beta})(s)

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathsf{tr}(\boldsymbol{\alpha})}\mathcal{L}_{\mathsf{fin}}(\boldsymbol{\beta})(s)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{\mathsf{PL}}(\mathsf{tr}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}))

H.6
Main Theorem

See
16

Proof.

It suffices to show that
\left\llbracket-\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{PL}}=\mathsf{tr}\circ\left\llbracket-\right\rrbracket
and
\mathcal{L}_{\mathsf{PL}}\circ\mathsf{tr}=\mathcal{L}
, which follows immediately from
Lemmas

82
and
86
. We then have:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathsf{PL}}\circ\left\llbracket-\right\rrbracket_{\mathsf{PL}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{\mathsf{PL}}\circ(\mathsf{tr}\circ\left\llbracket-\right\rrbracket)=(\mathcal{L}_{\mathsf{PL}}\circ\mathsf{tr})\circ\left\llbracket-\right\rrbracket=\mathcal{L}\circ\left\llbracket-\right\rrbracket

∎
